# jim-mini — release notes

Every release published to <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases>, newest first. GitHub keeps these in its own database, not in the repository; this page is the copy that travels with a clone.

**267 releases.**

## app-v3.1.4 — JIM-mini 3.1.4 — a screenshot is the whole screen

- Published: 2026-09-03
- Commit: `4adc90b0f60bf5ee139bf7ea748dc63054018b7a`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.1.4>

> A screenshot is the whole screen.
>
> The console is a fixed-height shell whose content column scrolls, so a full-page capture measured one phone height whatever the screen actually held. The camera unrolls the column before the shutter, so a capture is the whole screen; screens taller than the glass are also sliced a phone height at a time (72 slices), listed in a gallery section written from what is on disk. The count guard learns that a slice is not a screen and holds the slices to their own rule.
>
> Cut with the siblings — the three products keep one number
>

## app-v3.1.1 — JIM-mini 3.1.1 — the image carries what the box runs

- Published: 2026-09-03
- Commit: `dae35953bcb779e9eb9edcadad577c7c6113ea00`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.1.1>

> The image carries what the box runs.
>
> With the hosted cloud's profiles in place, the first probe on the VPS got through every wall — the namespaces raised, the hidden places mounted, the interpreter running inside — and fell on `import pytest`: the runtime image installed only what the server needs. The image installs the dev extra now, on purpose, because the box runs the tests a drafted edit names inside the container. And the tree the box copies is named by JIM_SOURCE_DIR, which the image sets to /srv: the package the server runs from is the installed one in site-packages, and its parent is not a tree. A test reads the Dockerfile for both.
>
> Cut with QRME and PDI at 3.1.1 so the three products keep one number
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.1.0...app-v3.1.1

## app-v3.1.0 — JIM-mini 3.1.0 — one number across the three

- Published: 2026-09-03
- Commit: `3fcd4b36bb65b3d8f3ce9e92ea0d90e8657d6722`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.1.0>

> One number across the three, and the box on a busy server.
>
> **One number across the three.** JIM-mini, QRME and PDI are cut together at 3.1.0, the way mechanism §7 of the filing describes them, so a box carrying the three products carries one version.
>
> **The box's process ceiling is headroom, not a flat count.** The kernel counts every task the run's user already has against the process limit, the server's own threads included, so a flat ceiling of thirty-two was tripped by a forty-thread server before the box's first fork. The ceiling is now what the run's user has plus thirty-two of headroom, read at the moment of the run; the probe forks past the headroom to prove the count holds, and a test starts forty-eight threads of its own before trying a draft.
>
> **What the hosted cloud needs, written down.** The hosting page says what a container must allow for the box to open — unshare and mount past Docker's default seccomp profile, mounts and user namespaces past its default AppArmor profile — and points at the profiles the compose stack ships for exactly that. A container that allows none of it gets the same sentence as before.
>
> Suite: 3334 passed, plus the release-field guard green after the build-code fix
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.11...app-v3.1.0

## app-v3.0.11 — JIM-mini 3.0.11 — the coding assistant gets a box

- Published: 2026-09-03
- Commit: `e4ff97427a16277f0fd657045b13c40645aac00e`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.0.11>

> The coding assistant gets a box, and the line answers.
>
> **The line answers (3.0.10).** A contact the cascade rang who rings the number back inside a day reaches the conversation about that reach-out: a leg of its own, consented by the act of calling, opened with what they are calling back about, then the same grounded turns every leg takes. Anyone else hears one fixed sentence in their language, and nothing is kept but the audit. A call back never advances the cascade, and a cascade a call back reached rings nobody more. The posture says whether the number is pointed at the line, and the Safety screen prints the two URLs to point it with.
>
> **The coding assistant's box (3.0.11).** A drafted app edit is tried before a person is asked to judge it: the diff applied to a copy of the tree (never the database or a secret), the tests it names run with the network cut, every life on the disk hidden, the run pid 1 of its own pid namespace so nothing outlives it, processes counted, time, memory and output finite, the other rooms hidden, the tree read-only. A red run goes back to the assistant with what the tests said, for up to three tries in all, and every round is filed beside the diff, so oversight reads a fact and not a guess. A host that cannot raise all four walls gets a sentence instead of a button. The box decides nothing: an approved edit still rides the next publish-merge.
>
> **Reviewed twice.** Two adversarial passes found 36 real defects in the box, from the live database being copied into the room to a detached grandchild outliving the wall clock; all are fixed and pinned by 40 tests.
>
> **For examination.** The README carries the filing, the components, the seven mechanisms on file, the safety path as built, and where each highlight is proven; the console was photographed again from the current build.
>
> Suite: 3336 passed
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.9...app-v3.0.11

## app-v3.0.9 — JIM-mini 3.0.9 — the ticker

- Published: 2026-09-03
- Commit: `61d585e033c30b08abceb4fefaf8dc6906f2aafc`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.0.9>

> JIM's clock advances without a read.
>
> Every deadline used to move only when something looked: the crash watch re-asked and tripped when the console polled or a status door was read, the vigil checked the silence when its screen opened, and a placed reach-out call the phone line never reported on was settled by that same sweep. A collapse at 3 a.m. with every screen closed waited for morning.
>
> **One thread, the same sweeps.** Every thirty seconds by default, JIM sweeps exactly the users whose clocks are running through the same functions the reads call. A tick is a read nobody had to make; nothing new decides anything. One sweeper per person at a time, one writer on the audit chain.
>
> **Honest posture.** The crash watch's status carries whether the ticker is running, as the thread's fact, and the Safety screen says in words whether JIM checks on its own or only while a screen is looking
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.8...app-v3.0.9

## app-v3.0.7 — JIM-mini 3.0.7 — the learn task plants itself

- Published: 2026-09-03
- Commit: `7f36da86152ec89b0ed9ffb2e33da8e20b641921`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.0.7>

> PDI 3.0.1 gave the resident corpus.learn, and it waited on somebody planting the task by hand. The person's own switch does it now.
>
> **Capture on plants it.** A standing learn task lands in the vault, scoped to this person's bundles and nothing else, every day — one per person, never doubled.
>
> **Capture off takes it back.** The row lets go only after the vault did; a cancel that cannot reach the vault keeps the id so the next try can still end it.
>
> **The bank archives itself.** Every fifty examples, once a plan-gated vault is there, a bundle lands for the task to learn from — and the first archive plants the task for a person who never touched the switch.
>
> The posture and the Settings card say what stands, or why nothing does — no vault, an older tandem, an unreached one — in words.
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.6...app-v3.0.7

> **The app-edit review desk is the Oversight tab.** It shipped in 3.0.5 as two reviewer-token doors and no screen. Open it with the deployment's reviewer token (JIM_ADMIN_TOKEN), read each proposal with its target and its patch, approve or reject it with a note to the person who proposed it, and see what is approved and queued to ride the next publish. The posture line says which lane this deployment is — hosted cloud, every edit held here; or self-hosted, approved on arrival — and that approving queues an edit and never applies it to the running app.
>

## app-v3.0.5 — JIM-mini 3.0.5 — users can edit the app, and the model menu is a loadout per region

- Published: 2026-09-02
- Commit: `39a3375a25b251aabfcc75cee96d8945435b9553`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.0.5>

> **Users can edit the app itself — submitted, and held at apply.** A person proposes a change to the app, or has the coding assistant write one from an instruction, right in the Studio. Two lanes: free rein on their own server (JIM_SELF_HOSTED=1 — approved as it arrives); held for company oversight on the hosted cloud, decided through reviewer-token doors (JIM_ADMIN_TOKEN). Either way an approved edit is queued to ride the next publish-merge — this seam never writes to the running app or deploys on its own.
>
> **The model menu is a loadout per region.** Twenty-two providers, each tagged with its origin — American (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, xAI, Meta, Microsoft, Amazon, Perplexity, Groq, Together, Fireworks, NVIDIA), foreign (DeepSeek, Mistral, Qwen, Kimi, GLM, Cohere), and the ones that never leave the machine. What an account is offered is its sign-up region's loadout — home providers first, then a curated few popular foreign. JIM_MODEL_POLICY=american tapers only the American-region menu, in one line. Anthropic leads as the beta default. Video generation shares the table (Runway, Pika, Luma, Veo, Sora, Higgsfield; Kling, Hailuo, Vidu, Seedance).
>
> **No company emblems.** The model tiles draw a generic monogram in one accent and label by public name. No rainbow.
>
> Tandem release across the trio at 3.0.5
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.4...app-v3.0.5

## app-v3.0.4 — JIM-mini 3.0.4 — the offline training corpus

- Published: 2026-09-02
- Commit: `1570603e6697abe1fbd4d3a500042347593dbbae`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.0.4>

> offline.py makes offline safe (nothing leaves the machine); this makes it capable. Every exchange the agents have — JIM, the coach, the mailbox, the reach-out cascade — is banked as a training example at the one place they all pass through (jim.llm.generate_for_user), so a local model trained from it grows able enough to actually answer offline.
>
> - **The person owns it.** Capture is on by default (their own data, for their own model, the memory-vault posture), but the switch stops it, purge clears it, archive seals it into the vault, and a full account erase reaches the training_examples table with no line of its own.
> - **Honest about what is wired.** The corpus banks now; a local language model trained from it and served when offline is the next step, and the posture says it is not wired yet — the corpus filling is what it waits on.
> - Doors: GET /corpus/{user}, PUT /corpus/{user}/consent, POST /corpus/{user}/archive, DELETE /corpus/{user}; an Offline-training card on Settings.
>
> Tandem release across the trio at 3.0.4
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.3...app-v3.0.4

## app-v3.0.3 — JIM-mini 3.0.3 — the moderated mailbox for the coach agent

- Published: 2026-09-02
- Commit: `aa9e90bc169f49f67a99099b69deaa65fbd5d0fb`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.0.3>

> The coach agent can now carry on email — read what comes in, draft a reply grounded in the thread, answer back and forth, and write to someone new — but nothing leaves without a person approving it.
>
> - **Held at the send.** Every outbound message is composed as a draft in a moderation queue; a person approves, edits, or discards it, and only an approval sends. It sends through the deployment's mail server when one is configured, and stages — composed and held, never dropped, never claimed sent — when none is (jim/mailbox.py, the same held-at-the-send shape the 911 dialer holds its send with).
> - **A voice chosen by role.** coach here — warm, brief, never diagnosing; a profession when this seam is lifted into a QRME synthetic profile. One place chooses the voice, so the lift is a role, not a rewrite.
> - **The Mail screen.** Take a message in, draft, edit, approve or discard, or originate one — every word through the console's ten-language table. Owner-gated doors, five catalogued audit actions, and inbound wiring (a provider webhook or IMAP poll) named as the next step in posture.
>
> Tandem release across the trio at 3.0.3
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.2...app-v3.0.3

## app-v3.0.2 — JIM-mini 3.0.2 — the reach-out operator screen and the crash-watch trigger

- Published: 2026-09-02
- Commit: `13f7e6515d8286f3d1204b751fd34515530cd69b`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.0.2>

> The reach-out cascade that 3.0.1 built as library and webhook now has a person-facing door and fires by itself when the person cannot answer.
>
> - **Reach-out operator screen (Safety).** The dialer's posture said plainly — the 911 line is built and held shut in source, and how a call would be carried (online through the chosen provider, or the user's own phone) once a transport is wired — the reach-outs that are running with each contact call's live status, and a control to start one by hand. Every word goes through the console's ten-language table.
> - **The crash watch reaches for people.** An unanswered critical reading now fires the cascade automatically: the trusted person first, then the account's emergency contact, alongside the email it already sent. The ticked emergency-services box becomes the cascade's life_threatening flag — the held 911 rung is the last rung, reached only once the contacts are exhausted. Nothing rings yet; each call is prepared and documented, and the send stays held shut in jim/dialer.py.
> - **Owner-gated doors.** GET /dialer/{user}/posture, GET /reachout/{user}, and POST /reachout/{user} join the provider's call-id webhook handlers.
>
> Tandem release across the trio at 3.0.2
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.1...app-v3.0.2

## app-v3.0.1 — JIM-mini 3.0.1 — the reach-out cascade, and the 911 door held shut

- Published: 2026-09-02
- Commit: `4fba5ce6e389e535d18ccb24878bf6514237338c`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v3.0.1>

> JIM can assemble and route a cascade of emergency calls to a person's trusted contacts, one after another: a keypad gate (1 to hear the message, 2 to never be called again), a language-model conversation grounded in what is happening, an opt-out that sticks, and a move to the next contact when one is not reached. When the contacts are spent and the situation is life-threatening, the last rung is the 911 dialer — assembled and routed, and the send held shut in source, openable by no setting, plan, or waiver. Nothing rings yet: every call is prepared until a telephony transport is wired, over a seam that carries calls both ways and names two kinds (online default on a selectable provider; the user's own device SIM). The cascade logic and the held dialer ship as tested library code; the provider webhooks are the only HTTP surface, and the operator screen and crash-watch trigger follow in a later round.
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v3.0.0...app-v3.0.1

> There are no functional changes to JIM-mini in this release.
>
> The round is QRME's: a room now holds the second key to what the synthetic
> people in it may reach — the owner's grant says what a profile can ever do,
> the room says what it may do there, for the people in it — and the AI and
> VERIFIED marks moved out of the image pixels onto the surface that draws
> them, because every face is drawn as a circle and a mark in the corner of a
> square is exactly what a circle crops.
>
> It cuts anyway, and says so, because a note that inflates an empty round
> teaches people to skim the ones that are not empty.
>
> Ten declarations, not the two that are easy to remember: a cut that bumps
> `pyproject.toml` and `package.json` alone leaves the three native shells
> reporting the last number a person can install and file a crash against.
> `versionCode` and the iPhone's `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` are derived from
> the version rather than kept by hand.
>

## app-v2.7.1 — JIM-mini 2.7.1 — the floats stop lying

- Published: 2026-08-30
- Commit: `07f71a6024ba43270b76b4aa57f229f772775b58`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v2.7.1>

> **Nothing that floats sits on a control any more, and the guards can see it.**
> Three rendering defects, none of which any test in this suite could see, because
> every one of them read declarations where the question was about rectangles.
>
> - **The lift had never once applied.** The phone rule raising the help button
>   and the status widgets clear of the tab bar was declared two hundred lines
>   above the base rules it overrides. Same specificity, later wins — so the
>   browser used the desktop rule, under a comment explaining the fix. The task
>   window had no phone rule at all and floated over the Hands screen's move
>   checkboxes, which are the controls that card exists to offer.
> - **The clearance was a guess.** `76px`, about a bar that is as tall as its
>   labels — translated into ten languages, wrapping sooner in the longer ones.
>   The bar measures itself now and publishes `--tabbar-h`, which `:root` also
>   declares for a browser without a ResizeObserver.
> - **The minimized light was an ellipse.** It rendered 22 wide and 44 tall: it
>   is a `<button>`, and the phone block sets `button { min-height: 44px }` so
>   every control is a real tap target, and `min-height` beats `height`. The
>   guard read the declared 22 and 22 and passed. The button is the tap target
>   now and carries no paint; a face inside it is the circle.
>
> **The screenshot harness builds the console before photographing it.** The
> build was a requirement written in prose in its own docstring, never checked,
> and this checkout's bundle was four days old — so a gallery could be re-shot to
> show a stylesheet fix and photograph a bundle from before it, looking exactly as
> convincing. The gallery here is re-shot on a console built from this tree.
>
> **Every numeric floor in the suite is registered and audited.** The last six
> left the backlog, and registering them found one that had been passing while
> measuring a different quantity on every run.
>
> A patch: no wire changes, no schema changes, nothing a client must be rebuilt
> against. Tested together with QRME 2.7.1 and PDI 2.7.1.
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v2.7.0...app-v2.7.1

## app-v2.7.0 — JIM-mini 2.7.0 — the Guardian gets hands

- Published: 2026-08-29
- Commit: `94ad25e0f68730ca7a12adefae6c2e58903840fc`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v2.7.0>

> The coach could already see, hear and speak, and could not press
> anything. 2.7.0 gives it hands.
>
> **A grant is something a person writes down** — which surface, which named
> apps, which moves, for how many minutes and how many steps — and one press
> takes the whole thing back. The bounds live on the server (`jim/hands.py`);
> the screen fetches them rather than inventing its own, and nothing moves
> without a small program the person runs on their own machine.
>
> - **Deliberately the sibling's machinery.** Verb for verb, bound for bound,
>   path for path with QRME's, so the motor on somebody's own machine does not
>   have to know which of two products it is talking to.
> - **Two doors, one row.** Pick the permission from the form, or say it in
>   words. The spoken door is strict on purpose: words that name no app grant
>   nothing, and the echo shows what the sentence was understood to mean before
>   anything moves.
> - **It looks before it presses,** through the eyes this product already had
>   (`jim/sight.py`). The screen is data to be read, never instructions to be
>   obeyed.
> - **It will not type a secret.** A password field ends the step rather than
>   being filled — by field name and by the shape of the text — and the ledger
>   records the refusal as the system working.
> - **What was permitted and what happened are two facts.** A landing is
>   reported by the only end that can see a cursor and lands in its own table;
>   a step nobody reported on reads as unlanded, not as a quiet yes.
> - **A body is not a screen.** The `body` surface can watch and describe and is
>   refused outright for acting, naming the four things a screen never needed.
>
> **Changed — the trio is back on one number.** Each README promises that one
> version names one tested combination of all three products, and three hands
> rounds cut in QRME alone drifted that apart. All three are cut at 2.7.0.
>
> **Fixed — the rule that lifted the help button off the tab bar had never
> once applied.** On a phone the sidebar becomes the bottom bar, and the floats
> are told to sit clear of it. That rule was written two hundred lines above the
> base `.help-fab { bottom: 22px }` — same specificity, later wins, so the
> browser used the desktop rule and the comment above the override explained a
> fix nobody was getting. The task window had no phone rule at all and floated
> over the Hands screen's move checkboxes.
>
> The overrides now sit after the rules they override, and the clearance is
> measured rather than guessed: the bar is as tall as its labels, which are
> translated into ten languages, so it measures itself and publishes
> `--tabbar-h`. `test_nothing_floats_over_a_control` reads the stylesheet for
> which rule *wins*, not for which rules exist.
>
> **Changed — the front-page gallery is photographs of the running console**,
> not drawings of it: 26 screens shot against a real backend, a real build and a
> real enrolment, by a harness that refuses to file a capture it cannot prove it
> reached.
>
> Tested together with QRME 2.7.0 and PDI 2.7.0.
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v2.3.0...app-v2.7.0

## app-v1.8.8 — app v1.8.8 — How old is the reading

- Published: 2026-08-27
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v1.8.8>

> How old is the reading, and am I allowed to act on something that old.
>
> - The staleness contract: readings stamped by the source's own clock, skew measured at ingress, unknown age treated as stale and never fresh. Every consumer declares its window — display 5 s, conditioning 30 s, escalation 60 s, trend 15 min — and past it gets a named state on the record. The wrist heartbeat rides apart from the readings, so the person going quiet, the phone sitting between, and the network going dark are three silences with three ladders. GET /freshness answers with the p95 age at the moment of decision — measured, not designed.
> - Meetings arrive as words: hand a recording to any open stretch and it is transcribed on the way through — the audio bytes stored nowhere, proven by a guard that sweeps every table — with the roster's own keeping rules deciding what survives.
> - The synced address book reaches all three native shells, behind the contacts grant.
>

## app-v1.8.1 — JIM-mini 1.8.1 — the phone's silent stop, and the voice on every surface

- Published: 2026-08-24
- Commit: `4040fc196d9283612f7be197a286d52d109c3fa9`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v1.8.1>

> ### The strip finds out when the phone stopped it
>
> A field report from an iPhone, against the sibling product: walk, swipe up to the home screen, come back to Safari, and the conversation had stopped without a word. It was fixed there. This console has the same strip and was never told.
>
>     asked     does the capture survive being put away
>     mattered  does the strip find out when it did not
>
> Every claim 1.8.0 made about an open capture surviving a hidden page is a desktop claim and an Android claim. iOS Safari suspends the whole page the moment you leave it and says nothing on the way out. Four places asserted the universal version, and **one of them was a guard banning the correction** — the belief could not be fixed without a test failing, which is what a guard is for when the belief is right and what it costs when it is wrong.
>
> `Listener` answers `live()` now, read off the recorder rather than remembered. Going away closes nothing; coming back asks, and says so in all nine languages when the microphone is shut.
>
> ### The voice, on every surface that speaks
>
> Reported from a Windows machine: *the voice is robotic again, it should be my voice when I'm talking to my AI*. The web strip was fixed in 1.8.0 and nobody asked the three native shells the same question. All three had it — `SpeechSynthesizer`, `AVSpeechSynthesizer`, `TextToSpeech` — each with a `speakAloud` in its own ApiClient, uncalled. The Windows client's summary had described the intended fallback for releases while the walk one file away never called it.
>
> All three reach the speaking service first now and fall back to the platform's voice, never the reverse: a built-in voice cannot fail, so putting it first makes the served one unreachable and nobody discovers it was configured.
>
> ### Guards that had stopped guarding
>
> Two of mine, caught this round:
>
> - An exemption recognising a no-op stub **by its exact spelling**. Adding a member to `Listener` broke it by *passing* — the substring vanished from every returned literal, so an exemption for one stub silently widened to all of them.
> - A comment stripper that treated `/*` **inside a string literal** as a comment, swallowing 54,296 characters of `Screens.kt` including the call it was hunting for. It had passed its own regression tests twice, measuring the right thing by luck.
>
> ### Also
>
> - The refusal ledger stopped describing 42 finished rows as outstanding.
> - `test_a_translation_in_the_wrong_script.py` — the backlog counted only *missing* translations, so it could reach zero with a Chinese row written in Cyrillic in it. Byte-identical in all three products.
> - `test_a_claim_about_a_platform.py` — holds any console to what it actually tested about a minimised window.
>
> Suites: **2726 passed, 5 skipped.**
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v1.8.0...app-v1.8.1

## app-v1.7.0 — JIM-mini 1.7.0

- Published: 2026-08-24
- Commit: `9f5c0d1343e5d05475a343ab898e5a17205c71f9`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v1.7.0>

> ## German finishes the informal register
>
> A German reader met *Sie* on the desktop console and *du* on the phone, and both registers inside each table. That choice is a claim about the relationship, not a synonym.
>
>     asked     is the string translated
>     mattered  who does it think it is talking to
>
> **381 rows** across the console and the three native shells are informal now, converted fragment by fragment so a partly-formal string could not be mangled by a global substitution. Every row still counted is named in the ledger as third-person `Sie`/`Ihr` — `Sie fragen` is *ask them*, `Ihr beide` is the informal plural — rather than left as an unexplained number.
>
> Two disagreements the guards found: three keys had been converted twice with different imperative endings, and `spec.ask` sat on the false-positive list while the console already said `Frag sie` for the same English.
>
> ## The refusal backlog counted sentences nobody can read
>
> The collector derives which exception classes to follow from the handlers that stringify them — correctly picking up `ValueError` — then swept every raise of that class, constructors included.
>
>     asked     is this sentence raised through a class a route stringifies
>     mattered  can a person reach the place it is raised
>
> `QRMEClient needs base_url or an injected client` fires in an `__init__` from environment variables while the app is wired: no object, no request, no reader. Seven rows across the trio, none ever owed a translation. The exemption is checked three ways, and the third caught my own first cut being wider than the thing it exempts from.
>
> ## A floor at a seventh of what it measures
>
> `assert len(i18n._REFUSALS) >= 21` against a table of **147**. Registered as a Ratchet so the comparison is made rather than assumed.
>
> Full suite green: 2672 passed, 5 skipped
>
> ## What's Changed
> * Informal register, and two refusal guards that were measuring the wrong thing by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/272

## app-v1.6.2 — JIM-mini app-v1.6.2

- Published: 2026-08-24
- Commit: `c3793c5b06426ea7f234b24922df912006a22bea`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v1.6.2>

> ## A script was being escaped like a page
>
> `_js_literal` builds the JSON and JavaScript literals the landing page drops inside a `<script>` element — including the translated string table — and it called `html.escape`. That is the right tool for a page and the wrong one for a script: **a browser does not decode HTML entities inside a script element**, so escaping there protects nothing and corrupts the value.
>
>     asked     is the value escaped
>     mattered  is it escaped for the place it lands
>
> `Terms & Conditions` reached the reader as `Terms &amp; Conditions`, in every language that ships. Anything carrying `&`, `<` or `>` rendered wrong.
>
> **The page was safe, but by accident rather than by its own mechanism.** The docstring named the real hazard exactly right: a literal `</script` ends the element whatever the JavaScript quoting says. The line written to stop it, `.replace("</", "<\/")`, sat *after* an `html.escape` that had already turned `<` into `&lt;` — so it never matched anything and never could. The protection came from the escaping that was corrupting the text.
>
> It is `json.dumps` now, with `<`, `>`, `&` and the two JavaScript line terminators U+2028/U+2029 written as `\uXXXX` — which JavaScript reads back as the original character and an HTML parser cannot mistake for markup. All three products carried the same helper and the same defect.
>
> ## Three rows in the escaping ledger were never defects
>
> The sweep behind `unescaped_markup.txt` scanned every f-string **twice** — once inside its own function, once in a pass walking the whole module — and reported the union. Each pass had a blind spot the other did not, so each became a permanent row: the per-function pass could not see a module-level constant, and the module-wide pass merged every function's assignments into one table, so a name written safely in one function and unsafely in another took the unsafe binding.
>
>     asked     is this interpolation safe
>     mattered  is the name being read the one that is written there
>
> Each function is scanned once now, in a scope seeded with the module's own bindings. The ledger goes from 3 rows to 2. A security record is read by somebody deciding whether to look closer, and the file says of itself that a record four-fifths noise is a record nobody reads.
>
> ## A Microsoft dependency pin read as one of this release's own version fields
>
> At 1.6.2 the release guard reported `<PackageReference Include="Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK" Version="1.6.240923002" />`, because `1.6.2` is a *substring* of `1.6.240923002`. Acting on the message as written would have been the worst available fix: adding that pin to `release_fields.txt` makes the next bump rewrite Microsoft's version. The exemption for a third party's number now covers MSBuild's `<PackageReference>` as well as Gradle's `group:artifact:version`, which collided on an exact match once before.
>
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v1.6.1...app-v1.6.2

## app-v1.6.1 — JIM-mini app-v1.6.1

- Published: 2026-08-23
- Commit: `bd7ace945fb57946ce08b8685ae711e69604401f`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v1.6.1>

> ## A mail server having a bad day could discard a health reading
>
> Three sends live on the monitor path: the calendar's reminder pass, the far end's monthly proof-of-mailbox, and the far end's own **alert**, which is reached from inside `guardian.monitor` itself. None of the three was wrapped — and that was reasonable when it was written, because with no mail server configured `mailer.deliver` prints the letter on the server and returns. It could not fail.
>
>     asked     did the letter go
>     mattered  did the reading get recorded
>
> Configuring a real mail host made it reachable, and the shape is severe. `POST /monitor/{user_id}` calls both sweeps **above** `guardian.monitor`:
>
> ```
> schedule.remind_pass(...)      # can now raise
> farend.liveness_pass(...)      # can now raise
> answer = guardian.monitor(...) # never reached
> ```
>
> So a refusal — or an SMTP connection hanging to its thirty-second timeout — raised straight out of the route. A reading taken because somebody's blood oxygen was falling was discarded with a 500, and no detection and no escalation ran, because the mailbox was unwell.
>
> The sibling rungs each catch their own send: the care beacon, the crash watch and the vigil have since they were written. The three on the monitor path never did.
>
> All three are caught now and report honestly rather than silently. A refused alert returns `delivered: False` carrying what the server said; a refused proof records a `farend_ping` event an operator can find. The proof still stamps `farend_pinged_at` on failure, deliberately — it rides *every* reading, and each attempt against a dead server pays the SMTP timeout, so an unstamped failure turns one bad mailbox into a slow request for every reading a wearer takes.
>
> **And the reminder repeated the far end's untruth.** `mailed` was set from having *called* `deliver`, so it was true for a letter printed on a server with no mail host — the same defect fixed in `farend` in 1.6.0, in a module that release never looked at. It follows the transport now.
>
> ## A transient mail outage could lock an address out of signup
>
> The account row is written and committed before the verification code is sent. An unhandled refusal there gave the caller a 500 while the pending account survived — so the next attempt from that address was answered *an account is already pending — verify the emailed code*, naming a code nobody ever received.
>
>     asked     did the code go
>     mattered  can this person ever sign up
>
> The self-repair path above it only runs when the transport is `console`, so this was reachable only on deployments with a real mail server: the ones where signup matters. Signup answers now with `code_delivery: "failed"`, and `resend` — the way back — answers rather than raising too, since a 500 there would close the only door out.
>
> Ten new guards, each sabotage-verified. Two were written because the guards found what the reading had missed: a third unwrapped send, and a test of mine that passed for the wrong reason because an enrolled user has no verified account and never entered the branch being asserted on.
>
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v1.6.0...app-v1.6.1

## app-v1.6.0 — JIM-mini app-v1.6.0

- Published: 2026-08-23
- Commit: `df47434d2e56bcc356456259d1cba35049958c7f`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v1.6.0>

> ## A letter printed on the server had been reported as a person notified
>
> `farend.notify` set `delivered: True` the moment it had *written* a letter. With no mail server configured, `mailer.deliver` prints the whole letter on the server's stdout and returns `"console"` — and that return value was assigned to a variable and never read. The escalation result carried `notified_emergency_contact: True` while the letter went to a container log.
>
>     asked     was a letter written
>     mattered  did a person receive one
>
> **This was live.** The beta host has no SMTP configured, so every far-end escalation there reported the emergency contact as notified.
>
> Three places state the opposite claim outright: `notify`'s own docstring (*True only when a letter actually left*), the comment above `notified_emergency_contact` in `guardian._deliver`, and `jim/db.py` on the `farend_alerts` table (*one row per alert actually mailed*). This rung was rebuilt once already, back when it meant "a phone number is on file". The module's own words are that *an honest empty room can be fixed; a pretend notification cannot*. The console transport was a second empty room.
>
> **Three changes.** `delivered` follows the transport, and a printed letter carries a note — in the wearer's language, ten of them — saying it was printed rather than sent, which an operator can go and fix. A printed letter is no longer written into `farend_alerts`, because that table is what the standing check reads: a console row rode as *the far end was already told* and would have suppressed the next real detection for half an hour, including the first letter that could leave once mail was configured. And the monthly liveness note, whose whole purpose is to prove a mailbox is real on a calm day, is no longer attempted with no mail server to prove it against — it rides the monitor sense, so it had been printing a banner on every reading and stamping `farend_pinged_at`, putting the proof that could work out of reach for another `PING_DAYS`.
>
> **Nine assertions across eight files were resting on the old flag**, which is the clearest measure of how far it had spread. `test_claims`, `test_extras`, `test_first_aid`, `test_life`, `test_pdi_tandem`, `test_sensitivity_baseline`, `test_tandem_starters` and `test_anonymity` all read `notified_emergency_contact is True` on a machine with no mail server, as a proxy for *escalation happened*. Their subjects are sound, so they take a new `mail_server` fixture and now say what they always meant: on a deployment that can send, the far end is reached.
>
> `test_anonymity` gained the assertion it was missing, and it is the one that matters most there — the letter to a stranger's inbox carries the chosen pseudonym and never the legal name. A stranger's inbox is the rung where a leaked name would be hardest to take back, and nothing had been checking it.
>
> ## The coach's context stops cutting a symptom in half
>
> QRME's wall learned this first — *a cut inside a word is the one outcome it refuses* — and this repository had not learned it anywhere, in the product where the material being cut is somebody's health. Collected context now clips at a boundary and says plainly that the rest of the note is not there, and that a negation or qualification may be in it.
>
> ## 170 of 256 released versions rendered as literal text
>
> A Keep a Changelog heading is a reference link: `## [1.5.0]` renders as the characters `[1.5.0]` unless a definition sits at the bottom of the file. Every version from 0.20.0 to 0.68.0, and every one from 1.0.0 to 1.5.0, had none — and `[Unreleased]` had no definition at all. `docs/releasing.md` has warned about this step for a long time on the grounds that nothing complains when it is missed. Something complains now: `test_every_release_heading_is_a_link.py` fails on a heading with no definition, on an `[Unreleased]` pointing at a stale tag, and on a definition for a release the changelog never describes.
>
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v1.5.0...app-v1.6.0

## app-v1.5.0 — app-v1.5.0

- Published: 2026-08-23
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v1.5.0>

> An iPhone can speak in a QRME room now: that console records and posts the
> audio where the browser has no usable speech recogniser, which also brings
> the browser's own echo cancellation and gives back the barge-in 1.4.1 had to
> trade away. What that round learned about interruptions, the Guardian learns
> here.
>
> Also in QRME, and not in this repository: a filing arrives as words. That
> reader follows the font's own `/ToUnicode` map and a re-arranged
> `/Differences` encoding, which is the difference between a patent application
> that reads and one that arrives as a filename — reported four times. When a
> document still cannot be read it now says which kind of unreadable it is,
> because a scan, a locked file and a font that code cannot follow want three
> different answers and had been getting one.
>
> ### Added
>
> - **The Guardian answering an interruption knows where it was cut off.** A
>   voice screen plays a reply piece by piece, and speaking over it hushes the
>   rest — so what the person is left holding is a PREFIX. The turn that
>   followed was built as though the whole answer had landed, and the Guardian
>   carried on from a place nobody arrived at, or took as read a caveat that
>   never left the speaker.
>
>       asked     did they interrupt
>       mattered  how much had they heard when they did
>
>   The piece boundary is the answer: everything before the sentence in the air
>   reached them and everything after it did not, and the piece being played
>   counts as heard — it started, and treating it as unheard would have the
>   Guardian re-say a sentence somebody had already had enough of. One function
>   reports it for all four voice screens, because the order is the whole
>   correctness of it: read the count before the stop, not after.
>
>   **Typing interrupts too.** On the two screens with a text box, sending a
>   question now stops the reply it interrupts. Somebody who watched an answer
>   head off in the wrong direction and typed rather than spoke wanted the same
>   thing the speaker wanted; until now the voice carried on over the top of
>   their new question.
>
>   Stored nowhere, deliberately. Unlike a room transcript — which is replayed,
>   so the fact belongs on the message — a coach turn is generated from the
>   current question. A recorded interruption would be replayed into every
>   later prompt, and the Guardian would keep accounting for one nobody
>   remembers making. It is context rather than an instruction: the model is
>   told what happened and answers the new question, because told to start over
>   it would spend the turn on the last one, which is exactly what the person
>   interrupted to stop.
>
>   The one path it does not travel is the specialist door: that answer comes
>   from a profile in QRME over the tandem link, and that door takes a question
>   and nothing else. Sent there it would be dropped rather than read.
>
>   Optional on the wire and ignorable. Absent means nothing was interrupted,
>   which is the ordinary turn, and every native shell keeps working unchanged.
>
> The number moves for all three, because one version names one tested
> combination of them
>
> ## What's Changed
> * The Guardian answering an interruption knows where it was cut off by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/271
>
>

- Published: 2026-08-22

> JIM-mini 1.2.0 — the chosen voice is the one a phone hears.
>
> A phone withholds autoplay unless the playback descends from a real press, and the grant lands on the **element** a person started. This built a fresh `new Audio()` per sentence, *after* the await on the synthesis fetch — so by then the press was long over and each new element carried no permission of its own. Every piece was refused.
>
> Here a refused piece falls back to the device's own voice, so nothing went silent — which is exactly what hid it. The voice somebody chose, paid an engine for, in some cases cloned from their own throat, never played a single piece on a phone, and the browser's robot read every reply. The product looked like it was working. That is why it took a person holding a phone to report it rather than a stack trace.
>
> One element now, opened on the first press anywhere on the page and reused for every piece. Arming is global rather than wired into each screen: five screens speak here, and a list of gesture sites that must all remember to call something ships with one of them forgotten.
>
> Two more, found while fixing it. Every reply leaked a blob URL per sentence — a blob whose URL is still alive cannot be collected, and a standing conversation makes a reply a minute. And with one element reused, `{ once: true }` left the two handlers that did not fire still attached: eighteen dead listeners by the end of a nine-sentence reply, with the next piece's `pause` resolving a sentence that finished a paragraph ago.
>
> Suite: 2590 passed, 5 skipped
>

> JIM-mini 1.1.0 — the microphone has an off.
>
> Most of this release is in the sibling repository, where the room somebody sat in got taken apart and put back the way they described it. What landed here is the one control that kept coming up in the same conversation.
>
> **The sphere can be deafened without being hung up on.** Mid-conversation with the coach, the only control on the veil was the veil itself — and tapping that ends the conversation. That is the right control for *we're done* and the wrong one for *hold on*.
>
> Muting stops the recorder rather than dropping what it hears: an ear still running and discarding is a microphone still open, and the whole content of the press is that it is not. It does not end the conversation — the standing turn is untouched, so unmuting re-opens the ear where you left it. It does not stop the sphere speaking either, because being unable to interrupt is a different complaint from being overheard. On both spheres, the coach's and the check-in's.
>
> Also in this cut: the Wear shell and the .csproj version triple move with the release rather than needing a follow-up commit, and the suite's guard-name floor was raised from 1050 to 1685 — it had fallen under half of the 2107 functions actually there, and a floor at half is decoration.
>

## app-v0.98.0 — app-v0.98.0

- Published: 2026-08-21
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.98.0>

> **The answer begins before it ends, and the house is listening.**
>
> The voice conversation got the week's field reports answered end to end: the reply is spoken piece by piece — the first sentence synthesised alone, every later piece fetched behind the one playing — so the wait before the first word no longer grows with the length of the answer; the turn ends after 2.5 quiet seconds; and the voice follows the earbud that is already connected, in and out.
>
> **Highlights**
> - **The answer begins before it ends** — sentence-pipelined speech; cutting Jim off still ends the whole reply, and a failed piece is finished by the device's own voice.
> - **The turn ends when the talking does** — 2.5-second silence window; the reply routes to the connected earbud (`setSinkId` where the platform allows, the mic request as the lever on iOS).
> - **The conversation can be interrupted, and silence is not a turn** — the mic is open while Jim speaks (echo-cancelled), and the energy gate keeps the transcriber from inventing words out of a quiet room.
> - **The standing ear** — the console listens for the cue words with its own switch on the lights row; nothing Jim says can testify about itself.
> - **The sphere reaches the check-in** — the specialist's sphere opens over the check-in screen and resumes the pre-interruption round, waiting for the tap the browser demands instead of sailing past a message nobody heard.
> - **The Baseline screen is the home of every limit** — vigil, sensitivity (the unreachable third level repaired), and money sliders: low-balance floor, savings goal, mandate caps, with real doors on iOS, Android, and Windows.
> - **Leaving the room ends the conversation** — all five conversation screens tear down on unmount; no more hot microphone under a screen that no longer exists.
> - Plus: the task window got its paint (and a CSS ghost-variable guard), the Photos menu opens photos, the bed sensor says who it notices, and the Docker image carries every import.
>
> Full detail: [CHANGELOG.md](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md)
>
> ## What's Changed
> * The image carries what the code imports by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/260
> * The conversation can be interrupted, and the standing ear by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/261
> * The baseline screen is the home of every limit by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/262
> * The sphere reaches the check-in by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/263
> * Leaving the room ends the conversation by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/264
> * Release prep 0.98.0 by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/265

>   answer. And the label tells the truth about the new behavior, in ten
>   languages.

## app-v0.84.0 — JIM-mini 0.84.0 — Everything running, in one place

- Published: 2026-08-17
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.84.0>

> Six rounds under one number, around one question: what is happening right now, and did the person agree to it.
>
> ## Everything running, in one place
>
> The field ask, said twice: *users will want a window to see what tasks are running.*
>
> Every engaged session, liaison, call, open microphone and switched-on monitor in one reading — beside what has been spent today, what the plan permits, what is waiting and what is already handled. The kinds and the reasons are closed sets and the server composes no prose, so four shells say it in ten languages rather than rendering a sentence somebody wrote in English.
>
> ## A link past the call now needs both people
>
> Extending a guardian-to-guardian link past the call was one person's decision, taken on the other's behalf. Now both sides say yes, and the yes is matched to the wording — so re-wording the task drops the other side's agreement rather than carrying it onto something they never read. A closed link answers 404 on every route rather than accepting a name for a task nobody is doing.
>
> ## What the coach noticed becomes JIM's problem, and only when it has to
>
> The free local stack answers first; the paid model is reached for only where it could not; `critical` is excluded from that pass entirely, because it belongs to a person rather than to a model. Running out of budget reports what went unhandled and carries on with the free work — the first draft refused everything, which is the wrong shape: it declined work that cost nothing because it could not do the work that cost something.
>
> It advises. It does not act unattended — that is a separate decision and has not been taken here.
>
> ## The day as it was taken in, and what survived of it
>
>     asked     is the day captured
>     mattered  does what survives match what was promised before it was switched on
>
> Cues are read **before** retention is considered, so a monitor holding nothing notices exactly as well as one keeping everything, and stores exactly as little as it promised. What was dropped says which rule dropped it. This is not a recorder: literal always-on recording would need the roster's promises rewritten, somewhere for the bytes that is not this database, and an answer on two-party consent.
>
> ## A room reads cues rather than keeping footage
>
> The field ask, in its own words: *monitor you from devices in your home such as cameras, speakers, looking for visual cues and verbal cues.*
>
> A fall, a call for help, slurred speech — each graded in the escalation vocabulary already in use and resolved once, each naming the reference behind its grading. **The sentence the cue was read from is never what is kept.** A monitor only yields cues its own senses can produce, checked against the roster rather than trusted, so a doorway sensor never reports slurred speech.
>
> It cannot tell whose cue it is. A room camera catches whoever is in the room; the roster already refuses to switch such a monitor on until somebody says the people in that space were told, and this does not improve on that claim or pretend to.
>
> ## Two people on one call, each with their own channel 2
>
>     asked     can two people each have channel 2 on one call
>     mattered  does each of them know the other's guardian is listening
>
> Half of this already worked: `mic.handover` is per person and always was, so two people on one call never needed permission from each other. What was missing is that nothing knew they were the same call. So a pair is a **disclosure** and that is the whole of it — no audio, no content, neither side's session, device or gain. It forms only between people already each other's contacts, and it ends the moment either channel closes, because a row that outlived the session it names would report somebody as listening after they hung up.
>
> ---
>
> On the console, on iOS, on Android and on Windows.
>
> 2313 tests passing, 6 skipped.
>
> **Full changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v0.83.0...app-v0.84.0
>
> ## What's Changed
> * Both people, or the call ends it — and one place that says what is running by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/218
>
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v0.83.0...app-v0.84.0

## app-v0.83.0 — JIM-mini 0.83.0 — The people in your phone

- Published: 2026-08-17
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 0
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.83.0>

> The field ask, in its own words: *the person they may be talking to may already be in their friends list, and an agent monitoring you making a phone call outbound will know who you're calling the moment you're calling, so all of that can be set up as it's happening.*
>
>     asked     who is on the other end of this call
>     mattered  did we learn it from something this person already had
>
> ## It arrives by a grant, not by typing
>
> Nobody retypes their address book into a second app. `contacts` joins the sources a person already grants, and the device's book is the truth: `sync` replaces rather than merges, because a merge quietly keeps people somebody deleted from their phone months ago.
>
> ## This is the grant that is mostly about other people
>
> Every other source is about the person granting it — their pulse, their calendar, their spending. An address book is almost entirely somebody else: a few hundred people who never chose this product, cannot see that it holds their number, and were not asked.
>
> That is `touches_others` at its maximum, and the same argument `jim/monitors.py` makes about a camera bolted to a hall. So it is off until somebody turns it on, one function is the only door to reading it, and withdrawing the grant **drops the book** rather than merely stopping the sync — one switch, not two, held by a guard. A copy kept after somebody said stop is the entire objection, made real.
>
> ## The far side's number is still not stored
>
> `oncall.open` reads it for the notice's language and drops it, and that stays exactly true. Recognition only ever reads: a call from a number nobody granted leaves precisely as little behind as it did before this existed, and what the call row gained is the id of one of this person's own contacts.
>
> ## The guardians find each other at dial time
>
> Where the book says the far side holds an account here, and the two are already mutual contacts, and the permit is on, the link opens with the call instead of waiting for somebody to press something mid-conversation. All three conditions were already checked by `liaison.open`, so dial time catches its refusals rather than re-testing them — two copies of one rule is how the two drift apart. A link that cannot open is not an error on a phone call.
>
> ## What the call leaves behind is the appointment, not the call
>
> Mom's case, and most of the book's: nothing on the other end to link to, and that is not a failure. What survives is the thing to be done at a time, filed under the person it came from, landing in the calendar everything else reads. There is nowhere in the schema for a transcript, a summary or the audio, which is the point rather than an omission.
>
> ## Free on every plan
>
> A wall at Basic was built and reverted: it broke the guard holding that free and Basic reach identical capabilities, and the feature is two-sided — it only fires when both parties have one — so a wall would suppress the density it needs.
>
> ## Fixed
>
> - **A source name nothing reads is a switch that does nothing.** `phone` went into `models.Source` beside `contacts`, declared for a call history no code asks about, so a person could turn it on and nothing anywhere would behave differently. It comes back when something reads it.
>
> ---
>
> This is the first half of the round. The book is in platform custody rather than the PDI vault, QRME's side is not built, and there is no client surface yet — all three follow.
>
> 2175 tests passing, 4 skipped.
>
> **Full changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v0.82.0...app-v0.83.0

## app-v0.82.0 — JIM-mini 0.82.0 — The coach runs the day

- Published: 2026-08-17
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.82.0>

> Seven rounds. The coach stops waiting to be asked, the guardian gets a seat on the call, and two of them learn to work together.
>
> ## Two guardians working together, quietly
>
> When both people on a conversation have one, the two can collaborate — and never on the line. The call carries voices; the guardians talk over the network, which also means none of this needs a phone: the same link serves two people in a room, on a video call, or in a thread.
>
> It forms only where both sides already knew each other. `circle._mutual` is the gate: an invitation is one direction, two make contacts, either side deleting theirs closes the door for both. Nobody publishes a directory, and one-sided contact reaches nothing — which is what stops a stranger's guardian calling yours.
>
> Silent, and readable afterwards by the person it works for. Two agents negotiating on a channel neither human can inspect are two principals with counsel who never report back, so the link keeps both halves split by side. Nothing crosses without the `speak_for_you` permit.
>
> What extends it past the call is not an agreement to stay connected but a task — something with an end in it. A person stopping it always closes it; the call ending cannot close what the task is holding open.
>
> ## An aid on the call, and the notice that goes first
>
> The notice is the one everybody has already heard: *this call may be monitored or recorded to better assist you*. It plays in the language the far side is likely to speak, read from their dialling code — Switzerland hears German, French and Italian; Québec hears French then English; Morocco hears Arabic then French; an unknown number hears English and Spanish. The number is read for that and dropped: never returned, never logged, never stored.
>
> Notice, not permission. Nothing waits for the far side to answer and there is no state for *they objected* — hanging up is theirs, exactly as it is on every support line. What is enforced is ordering: `oncall.may_listen` refuses until the notice has actually gone out, confirmed by whatever played it rather than assumed by the server that composed it.
>
> ## What the coach could not answer becomes what JIM goes and learns
>
> The coach on the device answers all day with no network and no key. When it cannot answer it writes the miss down — and now something goes and finds out, folds the findings where the offline stack reads them, and closes the question so nobody pays to study it twice.
>
> Refused until somebody allows it (`study_on_your_own`), and bounded: a few errands a day, counted from the ledger rather than a variable, so a restart is not a fresh day's spending.
>
> What the budget is really protecting is tokens today, not tokens afterwards. The coach is the part that works in a tunnel, holds the private half where no third-party model sees it, and accumulates for as long as somebody uses it. When a model turn costs nothing, the right change is that this pass runs more often — not that the coach stops being the thing that answers.
>
> ## Somewhere to plug the monitoring in
>
> Cameras, speakers, screens, bands, rings, patches, doorway sensors, the strip under a mattress. Every row says what it takes in, who else it reaches, and what stays behind — *it notices you fell* and *it keeps the video of you falling* are different agreements.
>
> Nothing that senses people who did not choose it is ever on by default, and a guard reads the table rather than trusting anybody to have read it. Switching one of those on is refused until somebody says the room knows. Always-on is a property, not a default.
>
> ## Beside somebody while they write
>
> Three remarks at most on a draft: something in this person's own records the draft does not mention, something the coach knows that it is not using, and a thing this product can do for the part they are on. Each carries its evidence — a hand-written pack, an excursion and a paid model turn are three different claims.
>
> The draft is read and dropped, by construction rather than by promise: the module imports no database and calls nothing that writes. And it decides nothing; applying a remark is the writer's own act.
>
> ## Fixed
>
> - **A built sentence lost its template inside the wrapper that carries it.** A `Templated` *is* a `str`, so every built sentence was caught by an isinstance check and looked up by its finished English. `MUST_BE_ONE_OF` went out in English from seven raise sites, in every language, and nothing said so.
> - **A templated refusal reached the reader as English.** The route passed it on with `str(exc)`, which drops the template. `i18n.raised` is what a route hands on now.
> - **One way out, and it sanitises.** The excursion path was written inline in `api.py` twice and the copies had drifted. Both go through `research.excursion` now, and a guard holds them there.
>
> ## Changed
>
> - The beta deploy page has a pointer here now. It is documented once, in QRME, beside the compose file it describes — copies of a page about one machine disagree the first time somebody fixes only the one they had open.
>
> ---
>
> On the console, on iOS, on Android and on Windows. 2151 tests passing.
>
> **Full changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v0.81.0...app-v0.82.0

## app-v0.81.0 — JIM-mini 0.81.0 — The sentence that forgot how it was built

- Published: 2026-08-17
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.81.0>

> ## The sentence that forgot how it was built
>
> `str()` on a `Templated` returns a plain `str`, which drops the template. A refusal built by `i18n.fill`, carried on one of this product's own exceptions and passed on as `HTTPException(403, str(exc))`, therefore reaches the handler as bare English — in every language, silently, and looking exactly like a sentence nobody has translated yet.
>
>     asked     is the refusal translated
>     mattered  did it still know how it was built when it got there
>
> QRME shipped that defect on its sealed-dialer sentence: the one somebody reads while something is going wrong, translated into nine languages and reaching none of them.
>
> Nothing here launders a template that way today. That is worth keeping rather than assuming — the first exception in this product to carry a built sentence would otherwise ship the same defect with nothing to say a word.
>
> `i18n.raised` hands a refusal on in the shape it was raised. `test_a_built_sentence_is_not_laundered_through_str` reads which of a product's own exceptions carry a built sentence, then fails any route that catches one and passes on `str(exc)`. It is registered in `shared_guards.txt`, which stays byte-identical across QRME, JIM-mini and PDI.
>
> The guard found a second site in QRME the moment it ran — a route written in the same round as the fix, laundering a refusal exactly the same way.
>
> ## The shape of it
>
> 2,045 tests passing.
>
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v0.80.0...app-v0.81.0

> ## Added
>
> **The console gets a front door.** Twenty-four tiles and no way in: every capability sat behind one, and the tile carrying the mark opened a permissions panel rather than JIM. `Talk` is a composer at the bottom and a horizontal scrolling rail of features above it. The rail is a launcher, not a second implementation — every chip opens the screen the tile opened, same code and same guards — and its label is derived from the destination rather than listed beside it.
>
> **The Studio: tools you write, for yourself only.** A widget runs in a box with four walls, each a mechanism rather than a promise: the network cut with `unshare -rn`, one directory under node's permission model, no child processes, and capped CPU, memory and wall-clock. If any wall cannot be built, nothing runs at all — a sandbox that quietly degrades is worse than no sandbox. Seven routes, scoped at the door and again at the query; thirteen refusals in ten languages; limits fetched from the runner rather than written into the console.
>
> Console this release. The three shells are recorded in their own backlogs with the reason: a widget is written, not tapped.
>
> ## Changed
>
> Menu icons back to menu size; screens open at their top; Care Team conditions are blocks on a phone; the resuscitation waiver moves to Safety; the follow-up nudge names the condition and asks about a different one each slot.
>

## app-v0.75.0 — JIM-mini v0.75.0 — a record of what was done, and whether the record was edited

- Published: 2026-08-15
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.75.0>

> **The audit chain, ported from PDI.**
>
> JIM keeps the person's timeline in `events`, which gets pruned, reshaped and paginated — and every one of those is indistinguishable from tampering once the same rows are load-bearing for evidence. So PDI's hash-chained log is ported here: each entry's SHA-256 covers the one before it, and a retroactive edit or a deleted row breaks the chain and `verify()` says at which sequence number.
>
> Seventeen consequential acts are catalogued and all seventeen wired — the safety path end to end, a money mandate granted or withdrawn, a permission area opened or closed, an engaged session acting or taking it back, and the two ends of a life. The table carries no `REFERENCES users(id)`, because a chain a cascade can empty is not tamper-evident. An erase records itself rather than removing what the chain already said.
>
> `GET /audit/{user_id}` is the door that makes that honest: the person reads their own rows, the catalogue of what would be recorded, and whether the chain still hashes to itself. On all four clients.
>
> **The console-untranslated ceiling goes from 93 to 2.** Ninety-one strings across seventeen screens now carry a key and ten translations. Three of them were not translation work: an `aria-label` left in English beside a label that was too, a sentence built from two adjacent literals that no translator could reorder, and a component with no language binding because until now it had nothing to say. The two that stay are `ElevenLabs` and `OpenAI` — company names, printed because that is what the provider is called in every language.
>
> Cut with qrme and pdi on one number.
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v0.74.0...app-v0.75.0

## app-v0.74.0 — app-v0.74.0 — The whole mark, and the path where nobody can speak

- Published: 2026-08-15
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.74.0>

> ABRACADABRA carried an alchemical glyph; it carries the jim-mini OS lockup now, at a size the mark can be read at, with every other menu icon raised to match — on the console and all three native shells.
>
> The crash watch is the one way this product summons help when the person cannot press anything, and it was the only path that never went near the escalation ladder: it decided its own tier with a boolean and left no reasoning on the record. It goes through the ladder now and answers the same tier — deliberately, because this is the existing behaviour with its argument attached. And every page it sends now ends by telling the one person who can reach a dispatcher that JIM cannot — on both settings of the box, because a dispatch request relayed to connected systems is what most looks like a call having been placed.
>
> Also: settings you change by saying so, a grant list that switches off again, and three undos that would have failed at the moment somebody used them.
>
> One of three products cut together at 0.74.0. Full detail in CHANGELOG.md.

## app-v0.73.0 — JIM-mini 0.73.0 — the phones that never asked which backend, and the fallback that was never reachable

- Published: 2026-08-14
- Commit: `main`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.73.0>

> The phones never asked which backend they were talking to. `/health` has answered a version since a stale backend first cost somebody an evening, and the console has compared it against its own build ever since — but the native shells can be pointed at that same address and said nothing at all. The answer was on the wire the whole time and every shell decoded it away: nine places where the field was in the response and out of the struct. A phone pointed at an older install looked perfectly alive while every newer screen said "Not Found" for no stated reason. All nine read it now, and raise a dismissible banner naming both versions and the address — above the tab bar and above the welcome flow both, because a stale backend breaks the screens a signed-out person meets first. Ten languages, dismissed per launch rather than remembered.
>
> The device recogniser had never once run in a browser. Safari permits speech recognition to start only inside the tap that asked for it, and reading the voice settings first spent that gesture on a fetch — so the fallback promised in that module's header was refused every time, with no prompt and nothing the person could do. The flag whose whole job is to make the retry skip that failure was read after the same await, so the retry died in the same place. Everything decidable without the network is decided before the first await now.
>
> And a platform refusal printed its error code and stopped there. Being asked and declined is a different situation from no prompt ever appearing, and only one of them can be fixed by tapping again. The second now names the remedy and where it lives in Settings.
>
> This release folds in 0.72.2, which was prepared here but never tagged and never cut by the sibling products.
>
> Cut in step with QRME 0.73.0 and PDI 0.73.0.
>
> **Full Changelog**: https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/compare/app-v0.72.1...app-v0.73.0

## app-v0.72.1 — JIM-mini app-v0.72.1

- Published: 2026-08-14
- Commit: `dbb8ffd296b169ed82a9920194ecf4826bd702ff`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.72.1>

> Two answers the key check could not give. Both found in the field on the first night `POST /settings/voice/check` existed.
>
> ### A pasted key took the check down with a 500
>
> A credential copied from a dashboard arrives with a trailing newline. It went into an HTTP header verbatim, where `http.client` refuses it with `ValueError: Invalid header value` — before the request leaves the machine. That is not a `URLError`, so it escaped both handlers and the route answered `server_error`: the one answer nobody can act on, on the screen whose whole job is to say what to do about a key.
>
> Three of the four clients trimmed their input. iOS, Android and Windows did; the console did not — and the console is the client a deployment is configured from, so the only untrimmed path was the one that gets used. The value is stripped now where it is stored and where it is read, not only at the form: the environment half never passes through a form, and a `.env` line carries whitespace as readily as a paste does.
>
> ### An unpaid account read as a bad key
>
> ElevenLabs answers **401** to a subscription with a failed invoice — the same status it answers to a credential it has never seen. The verdict classifier asked only whether there had been an HTTP error, so a working key came back `key.refused`, whose sentence tells its owner to paste it again or make a new one. Sending somebody hunting a credential that was never the problem is worse than saying nothing, because the actual remedy goes unmentioned. `key.unpaid` is its own verdict now, in ten languages on all four clients, and it names the invoice.
>
> ---
>
> JIM-mini only this time — QRME and PDI stay on 0.72.0.
>
> Green over the tagged tree: 1918 passed, 4 skipped.
>

## app-v0.71.0 — JIM-mini app-v0.71.0

- Published: 2026-08-14
- Commit: `ae5a8ebdf3af8991e9a51fdb4143e81cf08aaba8`
- Assets: 8
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/releases/tag/app-v0.71.0>

> ## The session you leave running
>
> Until now this product had one conversational door, `POST /coach/{user_id}`,
> and it was a *turn*: you said something, it said something, and nothing was
> left holding. Which model answered depended on `PUT /model/{user_id}`, so "the
> online one" and "the offline one" were the same route with a different
> provider behind it — distinguishable only by the amber banner when a turn
> degraded.
>
> `jim/engaged.py` is the thing that was missing. `POST /engaged/{user_id}`
> opens a session; it stays open across turns and through the app being closed,
> and ends when you sign off rather than when you stop typing.
>
> **It does things rather than describing them.** Twenty-six tools — writing a
> journal entry, setting or moving a goal, starting or ticking a habit, adding a
> medication, booking something, changing how it talks to you, changing which
> model answers — each one going through the app's own door with *your*
> credential forwarded, so its reach is exactly yours and no longer-lived.
> `GET /engaged/reach` lists them in sentences, carries no token, and is exempt
> from the plan gate: how somebody decides whether to open one is not the thing
> being sold.
>
> **Everything it changes can be taken back.** Every acting row declares its own
> inverse as data — a `remove` that deletes what was created, or a `replace`
> that reads the prior value *before* the write and replays it after.
> `GET /engaged/{user_id}/acts` is the trail and
> `POST /engaged/{user_id}/acts/{act_id}/undo` is the way back. One act cannot
> be taken back and says so in words: putting your question to a QRME specialist
> sends your own words outside this product, and nothing here can unsay that.
>
> **Signing off is a handover, not a close.** What the session was about is
> deposited into the store the offline stack predicts from — the same path a
> paid coach turn already takes — and anything named on the way out becomes a
> standing watch: carried into every offline coach answer, and raised unprompted
> by `presence.beat` above everything the product noticed by itself.
>
> What it may **never** touch is a carve-out enforced against the registry
> rather than trusted to review: emergencies, alarms, escalation, the vigil, the
> crash watch, the beacon, the referral release, every money path, membership,
> erasure, the synthetic self, and anything belonging to another person. Those
> are refused by absence — the agent cannot call what is not in the list — and
> `engaged.NEVER` is checked both ways, so an entry that matches no route fails
> the suite rather than sitting there looking like a protection.
>
> **Ways back that were missing long before an undo trail needed them.**
> `DELETE /journal/{user_id}/{entry_id}`, `DELETE /checkin/{user_id}/{id}`,
> `DELETE /goals/{user_id}/{goal_id}`, `DELETE /habits/{user_id}/{habit_id}` and
> `DELETE /habits/{user_id}/{habit_id}/log/{day}`. A person could write in their
> own diary and had no way to unwrite it; could record how they felt on the
> screen that feeds the crisis pipeline and had no way to unrecord it; could
> mark a goal abandoned and never remove it.
>
> **An engaged turn the offline model served says so instead of answering.**
> `coach.reply` degrades gracefully, which is right for a screen whose job is to
> *say* something. This screen's job is to *do* something, and the stub cannot
> ask for a tool, so a session on a box with no key would have answered in
> canned prose, taken no action, and looked exactly like one that had considered
> the request and decided against it. It now stops with
> `engaged.needs_the_online_model`, the session stays open, and nothing was
> changed.
>
>     asked     did the turn come back with text
>     mattered  did the model that can act answer it
>
> **All four clients, in the same round.** The console gets screen 109; the iOS,
> Android and Windows shells each get the same surface — the reach card above
> the button, the transcript, the undo trail, sign-off with its topics field,
> and the standing watches. Thirty-four strings in ten languages, emitted into
> Swift, Kotlin and C# from one source so the three tables cannot drift from
> each other. It landed everywhere at once rather than on the console first, and
> the reason is the feature's own bargain: full-act autonomy is defensible
> *because* every act is listed with the way back beside it.
>
> ---
>
> Cut with QRME 0.71.0 and PDI 0.71.0. Full local suite green over this tree —
> 1903 passed, 3 skipped.
>

> * Engaged sessions: the online Guardian you leave running by @davidsbianchi1984 in https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/216
>

## app-v0.70.0 — JIM-mini app-v0.70.0

- Published: 2026-08-13
